  • identmat-left-identity -- warrant proof: I_m P = P: the (i,c) entry is sum_j delta_ij P_jc, whose only surviving term (j=i) is 1*P_ic = P_ic (matmul-entry + entry-of-identmat + finsum-single- support + ring-mul-left-id). Proof structure worked out in identmat-build.scm.
  • identmat-right-identity -- warrant proof: P I_n = P, the right-handed mirror of identmat-left-identity (only the c=j term of sum_j P_ij delta_jc survives).
  • identmat-type -- warrant reference: IDENTMAT(A,n) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A).
  • elem-f-type -- warrant reference: ELEM-F(A,n,k,l) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A; matof-in-mat on the swap entry function).
  • matmul-type -- warrant reference: MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat + finsum-type).
  • matmul-entry -- warrant reference: (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
  • interval-card-in-nn -- warrant well-known: INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
  • matprod-summand-type -- warrant well-known: j |-> P_{ij}.Q_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> CARR A for P:MAT(m,n), Q:MAT(n,nn) (entry-in-carrier gives both factors in CARR A; MUL closes; CARR(RAG)=CARR A).
  • elem-f-ck-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{jc} = 0 for c=k, j/=l.
  • ring-mul-zero-right -- warrant well-known: a.0 = 0: symmetric to ring-mul-zero-left. Lang, Algebra II.1.
  • ras-id -- warrant proof: identity of a ring's additive group is the ring's zero.
  • finsum-single-support -- warrant well-known: If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
  • elem-f-ck-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{lc} = 1 for c=k.
  • elem-f-cl-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{jc} = 0 for c=l, j/=k.
  • elem-f-cl-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{kc} = 1 for c=l.
  • elem-f-co-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{jc} = 0 for c/=k, c/=l, j/=c.
  • elem-f-co-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{cc} = 1 for c/=k, c/=l.
  • entry-of-identmat -- warrant reference: (IDENTMAT A n)_{ij} = 1 if i=j else 0 (entry-of-matof on the Kronecker delta).
  • elem-f-col-k -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ik} = 1 if i=l else 0 (k/=l).
  • elem-f-col-l -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{il} = 1 if i=k else 0 (k/=l).
  • elem-f-col-other -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ic} = 1 if i=c else 0 (c/=k, c/=l).
  • matrix-entry-extensionality -- warrant well-known: Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
  • elem-f-rk-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=k, j/=l.
  • ring-mul-zero-left -- warrant well-known: 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group. Lang, Algebra II.1.
  • elem-f-rk-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{il} = 1 for i=k.
  • elem-f-rl-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=l, j/=k.
  • elem-f-rl-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ik} = 1 for i=l.
  • elem-f-ro-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i/=k, i/=l, j/=i.
  • elem-f-ro-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ii} = 1 for i/=k, i/=l.
  • tel-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-inf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • finsum-ring-distrib-right-gen -- warrant well-known: (SUM_z f z) * r = SUM_z (f z * r) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert with ring-right-dist; a |-> a*r is an additive-group endomorphism.
  • tel-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ring-carrier-closed-mul -- warrant proof: MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
  • finsum-congruence -- warrant well-known: If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)= FINSUM(ag,g,S). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH. Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
  • ter-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-gj-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • finsum-ring-distrib-left-gen -- warrant well-known: r*(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r * f z) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert, r(SUM_X f + f z0) = rSUM_X f + r*f z0 by ring-left-dist, then the IH. No commutativity used (a |-> r*a is an additive-group endomorphism).
  • ter-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • matmul-assoc-summand-type -- warrant well-known: the triple-product summand (c,j) |-> (P_{row,j} Q_{j,c}) R_{c,col} is a function INTERVAL(1,k) x INTERVAL(1,n) -> CARR A: the entries lie in CARR A (entry-in-carrier) and MUL closes on CARR A.
  • finsum-fubini-c -- warrant well-known: finsum-fubini with curried set/finiteness premises (AND-packaged), for fact-friendly forward use.

ringoid-rel-is-equivalence (trust: well-known)

proven modulo:

  • ringoid-neg-diff -- warrant well-known: -(a-b) = b-a, the additive-group identity in any ring. crs proves it for concrete rings; it does not reach a structure's abstract (ADD r)/(NEG r).
  • ringoid-diff-telescope -- warrant well-known: (a-b)+(b-c) = a-c, telescoping in any ring. Same abstract-ADD/NEG gap as ringoid-neg-diff.

rolle (trust: none)

proven modulo:

  • rr-lt-implies-le -- warrant well-known: x < y is (x <= y and x =/= y) by definition, so in particular x <= y.
  • extreme-value-max -- warrant reference: Extreme Value Theorem (calculus.pdf Ch 2.5, used in Rolle's lemma): a continuous f on the compact [a,b] attains its supremum. Sequentially: a maximizing sequence in [a,b] has a convergent subsequence (seq-compactness of [a,b]); its limit c is in [a,b] and f(c) is the max by sequential continuity.
  • extreme-value-min -- warrant reference: EVT (min form): apply extreme-value-max to -f; the argmax of -f is the argmin of f.
  • ccint-membership -- warrant proof: Separation: x in CCINT(a,b) = SEP(x in RR | a<=x and x<=b) iff x in RR and a<=x and x<=b, by the SEP membership kernel rule.
  • rr-le-cases -- warrant well-known: u<=v => u<v or u=v.
  • max-val-strict-interior -- warrant well-known: c in [a,b], h(a)=h(b), h(a)<h(c) => a<c<b (a strict max above equal endpoints is interior).
  • nary-minus-2 -- NO WARRANT
  • binneg-in-fun-rr -- warrant well-known: Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
  • binplus-in-fun-rr -- warrant well-known: Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
  • rr-lt-trans -- warrant well-known: Strict order is transitive: from x<y<z, x<=z by rr-leq-transitive and x=/=z (else y would be both > and < x).
  • rr-lt-diff-pos -- warrant well-known: u<v => 0<v-u.
  • rr-le-diff-nonpos -- warrant well-known: u<=v => u-v<=0.
  • rr-prod-nonpos-pos -- warrant well-known: u*v<=0 with v>0 forces u<=0.
  • rr-lt-diff-neg -- warrant well-known: u<v => u-v<0.
  • rr-prod-nonpos-neg -- warrant well-known: u*v<=0 with v<0 forces u>=0.
  • continuous-nonpos-right -- warrant well-known: A function continuous at th that is <=0 on a right-neighborhood (th,bb) is <=0 at th (sign preservation under continuity / one-sided limit).
  • continuous-nonneg-left -- warrant well-known: A function continuous at th that is >=0 on a left-neighborhood (aa,th) is >=0 at th.
  • min-val-strict-interior -- warrant well-known: c in [a,b], h(a)=h(b), h(c)<h(a) => a<c<b.
  • deriv-neg -- warrant reference: Derivative of -f is -f': f(x)-f(a)=phi(x)(x-a) gives (-f)(x)-(-f)(a) = (-phi)(x)(x-a), with -phi continuous at a and value -L.
  • nary-neg-1 -- NO WARRANT
  • rr-le-neg -- warrant well-known: u<=v => -v<=-u.
  • rr-neg-eq-zero -- warrant well-known: -u=0 => u=0.
  • rr-midpoint-between -- warrant well-known: a<b => exists m with a<m<b.

rr-complete (trust: none)

trusted code: arith, ineq -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

  • rr-is-metric-space -- NO WARRANT
  • rr-abs-bound -- warrant well-known: |x| <= c iff -c <= x <= c. The standard two-sided unpacking of an absolute- value bound; the form continuity/limit arguments use to turn |x| <= c into a pair of linear bounds.
  • nn-add-succ -- warrant reference: The recursion equation defining + on NN: a + succ(b) = succ(a + b). Peano. ASSERTED because number-systems.scm axiomatizes NN's + by its algebraic laws (comm/assoc/distrib/zero) and never relates it to succ, leaving the two symbols unconnected. Retire it by constructing + as the recursion it is.
  • nn-le-succ -- warrant well-known: k <= succ k on NN.
  • rr-pos-halvable -- warrant well-known: Every positive real eps can be halved: there is a positive d with d + d = eps. Standard (d = eps/2).

  • matrix-entry-extensionality -- warrant well-known: Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
  • elem-f-rk-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=k, j/=l.
  • ring-mul-zero-left -- warrant well-known: 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group. Lang, Algebra II.1.
  • elem-f-rk-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{il} = 1 for i=k.
  • elem-f-rl-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=l, j/=k.
  • elem-f-rl-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ik} = 1 for i=l.
  • elem-f-ro-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i/=k, i/=l, j/=i.
  • elem-f-ro-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ii} = 1 for i/=k, i/=l.
  • tel-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-inf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • finsum-ring-distrib-right-gen -- warrant well-known: (SUM_z f z) * r = SUM_z (f z * r) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert with ring-right-dist; a |-> a*r is an additive-group endomorphism.
  • tel-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ring-carrier-closed-mul -- warrant proof: MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
  • finsum-congruence -- warrant well-known: If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)= FINSUM(ag,g,S). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH. Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
  • ter-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-gj-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • finsum-ring-distrib-left-gen -- warrant well-known: r*(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r * f z) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert, r(SUM_X f + f z0) = rSUM_X f + r*f z0 by ring-left-dist, then the IH. No commutativity used (a |-> r*a is an additive-group endomorphism).
  • ter-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • matmul-assoc-summand-type -- warrant well-known: the triple-product summand (c,j) |-> (P_{row,j} Q_{j,c}) R_{c,col} is a function INTERVAL(1,k) x INTERVAL(1,n) -> CARR A: the entries lie in CARR A (entry-in-carrier) and MUL closes on CARR A.
  • finsum-fubini-c -- warrant well-known: finsum-fubini with curried set/finiteness premises (AND-packaged), for fact-friendly forward use.
  • ring-neg-in-carr -- warrant well-known: NEG closes on the carrier (op NEG CARR CARR).
  • elem-g-type -- warrant reference: ELEM-G(A,n,r,k,l) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A when r in CARR A (entries are ONE/ZERO/r, all in CARR A; matof-in-mat).
  • elem-g-entry-l-vanish -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{jc} = 0 when c=l and j is neither l (diagonal) nor k (r-slot).
  • finsum-two-support -- warrant well-known: If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j in {i0,i1} (i0/=i1), then FINSUM(ag,f,S) = f(i0) * f(i1) in ag.
  • elem-g-entry-l-at-l -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{lc} = 1 when c=l (the diagonal entry of column l).
  • elem-g-entry-l-at-k -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{kc} = r when c=l and k/=l (the r-slot in column l).
  • ras-op -- warrant proof: operation of a ring's additive group is the ring's addition.
  • elem-g-entry-off-l-off-diag -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{jc} = 0 when c/=l and j/=c (off the diagonal, off the r-slot).
  • elem-g-entry-off-l-diag -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{cc} = 1 when c/=l (the diagonal, r-slot inactive).
  • elem-g-col-l -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{il} = 1 if i=l, r if i=k, else 0 (column l: diagonal + the r-slot).
  • elem-g-col-k -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{ik} = 1 if i=k else 0, when k/=l (column k is an identity column).
  • ring-one-in -- warrant well-known: ONE lies in the carrier.
  • ring-add-right-id -- warrant well-known: a + 0 = a (ring-add-left-id + comm).
  • ring-add-right-inv -- warrant well-known: a + (-a) = 0 (ring-add-left-inv + comm).
  • elem-g-col-other -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{ic} = 1 if i=c else 0, when c/=l (any non-l column is identity).
  • ring-neg-neg -- warrant well-known: -(-r) = r in any ring: r's additive inverse's inverse is r (group double-inverse).
  • nn-succ-le-antisym -- warrant well-known: succ a <= b => not(b <= a) for a,b in NN: succ a <= b gives a < b, so b <= a would give a < a. Elementary order on NN.
  • nn-le-succ-cases -- warrant well-known: j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).
  • nn-le-imp-neq-succ -- warrant well-known: j <= k => j /= succ k for j,k in NN (succ k > k >= j).
  • elem-g-rk-vanish -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=k, j/=k, j/=l.
  • elem-g-rk-at-k -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{ik} = 1 for i=k (diagonal).
  • elem-g-rk-at-l -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{il} = r for i=k (the E[k,l] entry).
  • elem-g-ro-off -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i/=k, j/=i (identity row).
  • elem-g-ro-at -- warrant reference: G[r,k,l]_{ii} = 1 for i/=k (identity row diagonal).
  • submat-type -- warrant reference: SUBMAT(P,p,q) in MAT(p,q,CARR A) for P in MAT(succ p, succ q, CARR A): each block entry is P_{succ i, succ j} in CARR A (entry-in-carrier; succ i in [1,succ p] for i in [1,p]), so matof-in-mat applies.
  • border-type -- warrant reference: BORDER(A,b,M,p,q) in MAT(succ p, succ q, CARR A): each tabulator value is b (in CARR A), ZERO A (in CARR A), or b block entry M_{i-1,j-1} (entry-in-carrier, i-1 in [1,p] for i in [2,succ p]); matof-in-mat applies.
  • border-entry-11 -- warrant reference: BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,1} = b (i=j=1 branch).
  • border-entry-1j -- warrant reference: BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{1,j} = 0 for j /= 1 (i=1, j/=1 branch).
  • border-entry-i1 -- warrant reference: BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,1} = 0 for i /= 1 (i/=1, j=1 branch).
  • border-entry-block2 -- warrant reference: BORDER(A,b,M,p,q)_{i,j} = M_{i-1,j-1} for i,j /= 1 in the border range (the i/=1,j/=1 branch of the def; NN-MINUS = monus predecessor).
  • pred-in-interval -- warrant well-known: i in [2, succ p] => i-1 in [1, p]: the monus predecessor of an index past 1 lands in the block range.
  • entry-of-submat -- warrant reference: SUBMAT(P,p,q)_{ij} = P_{succ i, succ j} for i in [1,p], j in [1,q] (entry-of-matof on the block tabulator, beta-reduced).
  • succ-nn-minus-1 -- warrant well-known: succ(i-1) = i for i >= 1 (monus by 1 then succ is the identity on [1,inf)).
  • funcomp-succ-type -- warrant well-known: z |-> f(succ z) : [1,q] -> X when f : [1,succ q] -> X (succ z in [1,succ q]).
  • finsum-interval-shift -- warrant well-known: FINSUM over [1,succ n] = f(1) . FINSUM over [1,n] of (z|->f(succ z)): peel the first term (finsum-insert-ag at k=1, X=[2,succ n]) then reindex the tail by the succ bijection [1,n]->[2,succ n] (finsum-reindex-ag). Standard finite-sum front-peel, same status as finsum-ord-peel.
  • succ-in-interval -- warrant well-known: z in [1,q] => succ z in [1, succ q] (2 <= succ z <= succ q).
  • succ-not-one -- warrant well-known: z in [1,q] => succ z >= 2, so succ z /= 1.
  • nn-minus-succ-1 -- warrant well-known: NN-MINUS(succ z, 1) = z: 1 <= succ z, so the monus is (succ z) - 1 = z (nn-minus-def + bt-succ-minus-1).
  • finsum-type -- warrant informal: Closure of the finite sum on the carrier. Induction on card(S): the fold seeds at (IDEN ag), in (CARR ag) by the identity law, and each step applies (OPR ag), which closes on (CARR ag) by the group's binary-operation typing. No inverses used -- the same argument as finsum-comm-monoid-type.
  • ras-carr -- warrant proof: carrier of a ring's additive group is the ring's carrier.
  • nn-minus-1-inj -- warrant well-known: i /= j and 1 <= i,j => i-1 /= j-1 (monus by 1 is injective on [1,inf)).

smith-staircase (trust: well-known)

trusted code: crs -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

  • identmat-left-identity -- warrant proof: I_m P = P: the (i,c) entry is sum_j delta_ij P_jc, whose only surviving term (j=i) is 1*P_ic = P_ic (matmul-entry + entry-of-identmat + finsum-single- support + ring-mul-left-id). Proof structure worked out in identmat-build.scm.
  • identmat-right-identity -- warrant proof: P I_n = P, the right-handed mirror of identmat-left-identity (only the c=j term of sum_j P_ij delta_jc survives).
  • identmat-type -- warrant reference: IDENTMAT(A,n) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A).
  • nn-zero-le -- warrant well-known: 0 is the least natural number.
  • nn-not-le-zero-pos -- warrant well-known: 1 <= j => not(j <= 0) for j in NN (0 is least, and j >= 1 > 0).
  • nn-one-in -- warrant well-known: 1 = succ 0 in NN.
  • nn-pos-is-succ -- warrant well-known: a positive nat is a successor (n>=1 => n = succ(n-1), n-1 in NN).
  • one-in-interval -- warrant well-known: 1 in [1, succ n] (1 <= 1 <= succ n).
  • entry-in-carrier -- warrant well-known: The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
  • gauge-is-degree -- warrant proof: GAUGE(s) = CHOICE(EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES s) is in FUN(CARR s, NN) and has division- with-remainder: IS-EUCLIDEAN-RING(s) makes EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES(s) inhabited (euclidean-ring-has-gauge + gauges-mem-build), so the epsilon pick lands in it (choice-axiom) and the SEP slices give both conjuncts. MACHINE-PROVEN in archive/calculus-pre-rename/gauge-proof.scm.
  • elem-f-type -- warrant reference: ELEM-F(A,n,k,l) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A; matof-in-mat on the swap entry function).
  • matmul-type -- warrant reference: MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat + finsum-type).
  • matmul-entry -- warrant reference: (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
  • interval-card-in-nn -- warrant well-known: INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.

  • nn-minus-1-inj -- warrant well-known: i /= j and 1 <= i,j => i-1 /= j-1 (monus by 1 is injective on [1,inf)).
  • nn-succ-mono -- warrant well-known: a <= b => succ a <= succ b.
  • interval-mem-intro -- warrant proof: Converse of interval-lo/interval-hi: i in NN with a<=i<=b lies in INTERVAL(a,b) (the right-to-left direction of interval-membership's SEP iff).
  • nn-one-le-succ -- warrant well-known: 1 <= succ n for every n in NN.

span-is-submodule (trust: none)

proven modulo:

  • ring-one-in -- warrant well-known: ONE lies in the carrier.
  • ring-neg-in-carr -- warrant well-known: NEG closes on the carrier (op NEG CARR CARR).
  • zeromat-type -- warrant reference: the all-zero m-by-n matrix is m-by-n over CARR A.
  • one-in-interval-1 -- warrant proof: 1 in INTERVAL(1,1): 1 in NN and 1<=1<=1. The column index of a column vector.
  • interval-card-in-nn -- warrant well-known: INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
  • matact-summand-type -- warrant well-known: j |-> P_{ij} . u_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> VEC md for P:MAT(m,n,CARR(SCAL md)) and u:MAT(n,q,VEC md): entry-in-carrier types both arguments, module-act-type closes the action, and mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
  • matact-entry -- warrant reference: (P.u)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij} . u_{jc}, summed in the module's vector abelian group; the read-off of MATACT's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof (dimensions m, n, q recovered from SIZE P, SIZE u).
  • mvag-id -- warrant proof: identity of a module's vector abelian group is the module's zero vector.
  • entry-of-zeromat -- warrant reference: (ZEROMAT A m n)_{ij} = 0 (entry-of-matof on the constant tabulation).
  • entry-in-carrier -- warrant well-known: The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
  • abelian-group-idempotent-is-id -- NO WARRANT
  • finsum-all-id -- warrant well-known: If f(z) = IDEN(ag) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S) = IDEN(ag). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term is IDEN and the rest is IDEN by the IH, so the sum is IDEN*IDEN = IDEN; the base case is finsum-empty.
  • matadd-type -- warrant reference: entrywise sum of m-by-n matrices is m-by-n.
  • mra-combined-summand-type -- warrant well-known: j |-> (c1_{1j}.u_{j1}) (+) (c2_{1j}.u_{j1}) is a function [1,n] -> VEC md: each summand is (matact-summand-type), and the vector abelian group's operation closes on its carrier (mvag-carr, mvag-op, module-vadd-type).
  • matadd-entry -- warrant reference: (P+Q)_{ij} = P_{ij} + Q_{ij}: the read-off of MATADD's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof + beta, with the dimensions recovered from SIZE P (mat-size).
  • mvag-op -- warrant proof: operation of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector addition.
  • finsum-congruence -- warrant well-known: If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)= FINSUM(ag,g,S). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH. Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
  • finsum-add-ag -- warrant well-known: finsum-add at m = ag as a commutative monoid: induction on |S| via finsum-insert-ag, regrouping (a+b)+(c+d)=(a+c)+(b+d) by commutativity and associativity of (OPR ag). No inverses used.
  • matscale-type -- warrant reference: entrywise scalar multiple of an m-by-n matrix is m-by-n (MUL closes on CARR A).
  • mrs-scaled-summand-type -- warrant well-known: z |-> r . (c_{1z} . u_{z1}) is a function [1,n] -> VEC md: the inner action is (matact-summand-type), module-act-type closes the outer action, and mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
  • finsum-act-distrib-gen -- warrant well-known: r.(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r . f z) in a module: x |-> r.x is an endomorphism of the vector abelian group. Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: r.(SUM_X f + f z0) = r.SUM_X f + r.(f z0) by module-act-distrib-vec, and the empty case is r.0 = 0, itself r.(0+0) = r.0 + r.0 killed by abelian-group-idempotent-is-id on the vector group (the module-zero-act route).
  • matscale-entry -- warrant reference: (r*P)_{ij} = r * P_{ij}: the read-off of MATSCALE's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof + beta, with the dimensions recovered from SIZE P (mat-size).
  • ring-add-right-inv -- warrant well-known: a + (-a) = 0 (ring-add-left-inv + comm).
  • abelian-group-inverse-unique -- warrant well-known: b = eb = (a^{-1}a)b = a^{-1}(ab) = a^{-1}e = a^{-1}, by group-left-inv, group-assoc, group-left-id, and abelian-group-opr-comm for the right identity. Every abelian group is a group (abelian-group-is-group), so all four are in scope.

spans-fg-base (trust: well-known)

proven modulo:

  • nn-zero-le -- warrant well-known: 0 is the least natural number.
  • matof-in-mat -- warrant well-known: The tabulated matrix MATOF(m,n,g) is an m-by-n matrix over X when every value g(i,j) (1<=i<=m, 1<=j<=n) lies in X.
  • nn-not-le-zero-pos -- warrant well-known: 1 <= j => not(j <= 0) for j in NN (0 is least, and j >= 1 > 0).
  • one-in-interval-1 -- warrant proof: 1 in INTERVAL(1,1): 1 in NN and 1<=1<=1. The column index of a column vector.
  • matact-entry -- warrant reference: (P.u)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij} . u_{jc}, summed in the module's vector abelian group; the read-off of MATACT's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof (dimensions m, n, q recovered from SIZE P, SIZE u).
  • interval-1-0-empty -- warrant well-known: [1,0] is empty: i in [1,0] gives 1 <= i and i <= 0, hence 1 <= 0, false in NN.
  • finsum-empty -- warrant informal: Mechanically proven before the 2026-05-27 PSS promotion: mac FINSUM exposes SUM-AG over CARD(EMPTY-SET), card-empty rewrites that to 0, and sum-ag-zero collapses the empty sum to the identity. Script archived at archive/proven-theorems-archive.scm (prove-and-install! 'finsum-empty). The archive predates the E -> IDEN accessor rename and the ==-sweep, so it is a record of the argument rather than a runnable script -- which is why this is informal' and not proof'.
  • mvag-id -- warrant proof: identity of a module's vector abelian group is the module's zero vector.

spans-fg-step (trust: none)

trusted code: crs -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: r.(SUM_X f + f z0) = r.SUM_X f + r.(f z0) by module-act-distrib-vec, and the empty case is r.0 = 0, itself r.(0+0) = r.0 + r.0 killed by abelian-group-idempotent-is-id on the vector group (the module-zero-act route).

  • unitrow-type -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i) is a 1-by-n matrix over CARR A (matof-in-mat; entries ONE/ZERO).
  • unitrow-entry-off -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i)_{1,j} = 0 for j /= i (entry-of-matof with the IF condition false).
  • finsum-single-support -- warrant well-known: If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
  • unitrow-entry-at -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i)_{1,i} = 1 (entry-of-matof with the IF condition true).
  • ring-add-closed -- warrant well-known: ADD closes on the carrier (curried form).
  • ring-neg-mul-left -- warrant well-known: (-a)b = -(ab): ab + (-a)b = (a + -a)b = 0b = 0 (ring-right-dist, ring-add-left-inv, ring-mul-zero-left), so (-a)b is the inverse of ab.
  • ring-carrier-closed-mul -- warrant proof: MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
  • gauge-is-degree -- warrant proof: GAUGE(s) = CHOICE(EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES s) is in FUN(CARR s, NN) and has division- with-remainder: IS-EUCLIDEAN-RING(s) makes EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES(s) inhabited (euclidean-ring-has-gauge + gauges-mem-build), so the epsilon pick lands in it (choice-axiom) and the SEP slices give both conjuncts. MACHINE-PROVEN in archive/calculus-pre-rename/gauge-proof.scm.
  • principal-ideal-membership -- NO WARRANT
  • ring-add-right-id -- warrant well-known: a + 0 = a (ring-add-left-id + comm).
  • nn-succ-le-antisym -- warrant well-known: succ a <= b => not(b <= a) for a,b in NN: succ a <= b gives a < b, so b <= a would give a < a. Elementary order on NN.
  • ring-mul-zero-left -- warrant well-known: 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group. Lang, Algebra II.1.
  • snoc-col-type -- warrant reference: [w_1..w_n, x] is a (succ n)-by-1 matrix over X: matof-in-mat, each value being x in X (row succ n) or ENTRY w i 1 in X for i in [1,n] (entry-in-carrier).
  • nn-succ-mono -- warrant well-known: a <= b => succ a <= succ b.
  • nn-le-succ-cases -- warrant well-known: j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).
  • entry-of-snoc-col -- warrant reference: SNOC-COL(w,n,x)_{i,1} = w_{i,1} for i in [1,n]: entry-of-matof (i in [1,succ n] since i<=n<=succ n, and 1 in [1,1]), then the IF is false since i<=n<succ n.
  • snoc-col-last -- warrant reference: SNOC-COL(w,n,x)_{succ n,1} = x: entry-of-matof (succ n in [1,succ n]), IF true.
  • matact-type -- warrant reference: P.u is an m-by-q matrix of vectors for P an m-by-n scalar matrix and u an n-by-q matrix of vectors: matof-in-mat, whose values are FINSUMs in the vector abelian group, typed by finsum-type + mvag-carr.
  • matact-summand-type-le -- warrant well-known: j |-> P_{ij} . u_{jc} is a function [1,k] -> VEC md for k <= n: matact-summand- type's argument, restricted -- entry-in-carrier still types both arguments for j in [1,k] subset [1,n] (interval [1,k] subset [1,n] since k <= n).
  • finsum-interval-peel -- warrant well-known: FINSUM over [1,succ n] = (FINSUM over [1,n]) . f(succ n): finsum-insert-ag at X = [1,n], k = succ n, using [1,succ n] = [1,n] u {succ n} (interval-mem-intro / interval-hi + nn-le-succ-cases) and succ n not in [1,n] (interval-hi would give succ n <= n). Standard finite-sum back-peel, the twin of finsum-interval-shift.
  • entry-of-block -- warrant reference: BLOCK(P,k,l)_{ij} = P_{ij} for i in [1,k], j in [1,l] (entry-of-matof).
  • finsum-type -- warrant informal: Closure of the finite sum on the carrier. Induction on card(S): the fold seeds at (IDEN ag), in (CARR ag) by the identity law, and each step applies (OPR ag), which closes on (CARR ag) by the group's binary-operation typing. No inverses used -- the same argument as finsum-comm-monoid-type.
  • mvag-carr -- warrant proof: carrier of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector set.
  • abelian-group-right-id -- warrant well-known: a * e = e * a = a, by abelian-group-opr-comm then group-left-id (via abelian-group-is-group).
  • abelian-group-assoc -- warrant well-known: (ab)c = a(bc): group-assoc via abelian-group-is-group.
  • snoc-row-type -- warrant reference: [c_1..c_n, r] is a 1-by-(succ n) matrix over X (matof-in-mat, as snoc-col-type).
  • snoc-row-last -- warrant reference: SNOC-ROW(c,n,r)_{1,succ n} = r (entry-of-matof, IF true).
  • entry-of-snoc-row -- warrant reference: SNOC-ROW(c,n,r)_{1,j} = c_{1,j} for j in [1,n] (entry-of-matof, IF false as j<=n<succ n).

  • ring-add-right-inv -- warrant well-known: a + (-a) = 0 (ring-add-left-inv + comm).
  • abelian-group-inverse-unique -- warrant well-known: b = eb = (a^{-1}a)b = a^{-1}(ab) = a^{-1}e = a^{-1}, by group-left-inv, group-assoc, group-left-id, and abelian-group-opr-comm for the right identity. Every abelian group is a group (abelian-group-is-group), so all four are in scope.
  • unitrow-type -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i) is a 1-by-n matrix over CARR A (matof-in-mat; entries ONE/ZERO).
  • unitrow-entry-off -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i)_{1,j} = 0 for j /= i (entry-of-matof with the IF condition false).
  • finsum-single-support -- warrant well-known: If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
  • unitrow-entry-at -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i)_{1,i} = 1 (entry-of-matof with the IF condition true).
  • ring-add-closed -- warrant well-known: ADD closes on the carrier (curried form).
  • ring-neg-mul-left -- warrant well-known: (-a)b = -(ab): ab + (-a)b = (a + -a)b = 0b = 0 (ring-right-dist, ring-add-left-inv, ring-mul-zero-left), so (-a)b is the inverse of ab.
  • ring-carrier-closed-mul -- warrant proof: MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
  • gauge-is-degree -- warrant proof: GAUGE(s) = CHOICE(EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES s) is in FUN(CARR s, NN) and has division- with-remainder: IS-EUCLIDEAN-RING(s) makes EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES(s) inhabited (euclidean-ring-has-gauge + gauges-mem-build), so the epsilon pick lands in it (choice-axiom) and the SEP slices give both conjuncts. MACHINE-PROVEN in archive/calculus-pre-rename/gauge-proof.scm.
  • principal-ideal-membership -- NO WARRANT
  • ring-add-right-id -- warrant well-known: a + 0 = a (ring-add-left-id + comm).
  • nn-succ-le-antisym -- warrant well-known: succ a <= b => not(b <= a) for a,b in NN: succ a <= b gives a < b, so b <= a would give a < a. Elementary order on NN.
  • ring-mul-zero-left -- warrant well-known: 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group. Lang, Algebra II.1.
  • snoc-col-type -- warrant reference: [w_1..w_n, x] is a (succ n)-by-1 matrix over X: matof-in-mat, each value being x in X (row succ n) or ENTRY w i 1 in X for i in [1,n] (entry-in-carrier).
  • nn-succ-mono -- warrant well-known: a <= b => succ a <= succ b.
  • nn-le-succ-cases -- warrant well-known: j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).
  • entry-of-snoc-col -- warrant reference: SNOC-COL(w,n,x)_{i,1} = w_{i,1} for i in [1,n]: entry-of-matof (i in [1,succ n] since i<=n<=succ n, and 1 in [1,1]), then the IF is false since i<=n<succ n.
  • snoc-col-last -- warrant reference: SNOC-COL(w,n,x)_{succ n,1} = x: entry-of-matof (succ n in [1,succ n]), IF true.
  • matact-type -- warrant reference: P.u is an m-by-q matrix of vectors for P an m-by-n scalar matrix and u an n-by-q matrix of vectors: matof-in-mat, whose values are FINSUMs in the vector abelian group, typed by finsum-type + mvag-carr.
  • matact-summand-type-le -- warrant well-known: j |-> P_{ij} . u_{jc} is a function [1,k] -> VEC md for k <= n: matact-summand- type's argument, restricted -- entry-in-carrier still types both arguments for j in [1,k] subset [1,n] (interval [1,k] subset [1,n] since k <= n).
  • finsum-interval-peel -- warrant well-known: FINSUM over [1,succ n] = (FINSUM over [1,n]) . f(succ n): finsum-insert-ag at X = [1,n], k = succ n, using [1,succ n] = [1,n] u {succ n} (interval-mem-intro / interval-hi + nn-le-succ-cases) and succ n not in [1,n] (interval-hi would give succ n <= n). Standard finite-sum back-peel, the twin of finsum-interval-shift.
  • entry-of-block -- warrant reference: BLOCK(P,k,l)_{ij} = P_{ij} for i in [1,k], j in [1,l] (entry-of-matof).
  • finsum-type -- warrant informal: Closure of the finite sum on the carrier. Induction on card(S): the fold seeds at (IDEN ag), in (CARR ag) by the identity law, and each step applies (OPR ag), which closes on (CARR ag) by the group's binary-operation typing. No inverses used -- the same argument as finsum-comm-monoid-type.
  • mvag-carr -- warrant proof: carrier of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector set.
  • abelian-group-right-id -- warrant well-known: a * e = e * a = a, by abelian-group-opr-comm then group-left-id (via abelian-group-is-group).
  • abelian-group-assoc -- warrant well-known: (ab)c = a(bc): group-assoc via abelian-group-is-group.
  • snoc-row-type -- warrant reference: [c_1..c_n, r] is a 1-by-(succ n) matrix over X (matof-in-mat, as snoc-col-type).
  • snoc-row-last -- warrant reference: SNOC-ROW(c,n,r)_{1,succ n} = r (entry-of-matof, IF true).
  • entry-of-snoc-row -- warrant reference: SNOC-ROW(c,n,r)_{1,j} = c_{1,j} for j in [1,n] (entry-of-matof, IF false as j<=n<succ n).

spans-transport (trust: well-known)

proven modulo:

  • one-in-interval-1 -- warrant proof: 1 in INTERVAL(1,1): 1 in NN and 1<=1<=1. The column index of a column vector.
  • interval-card-in-nn -- warrant well-known: INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
  • matact-entry -- warrant reference: (P.u)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij} . u_{jc}, summed in the module's vector abelian group; the read-off of MATACT's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof (dimensions m, n, q recovered from SIZE P, SIZE u).
  • matact-summand-type -- warrant well-known: j |-> P_{ij} . u_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> VEC md for P:MAT(m,n,CARR(SCAL md)) and u:MAT(n,q,VEC md): entry-in-carrier types both arguments, module-act-type closes the action, and mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
  • entry-in-carrier -- warrant well-known: The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
  • submodule-finsum-closed -- warrant well-known: A submodule contains every finite sum of its elements: induction on |S| via finsum-insert (the peeled term and the rest are both in sm, and IS-SUBMODULE is closed under VADD); the base case is finsum-empty and VZERO in sm.
  • matmul-type -- warrant reference: MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat + finsum-type).

  • mal-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • mal-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • matmul-entry -- warrant reference: (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
  • mal-inf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • finsum-act-collect-gen -- warrant well-known: (SUM_z c z).x = SUM_z ((c z).x) in a module: r |-> r.x is a homomorphism from the scalar ring's additive group to the vector abelian group. Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: (SUM_X c + c z0).x = (SUM_X c).x + (c z0).x by module-act-distrib-scalar, and the empty case is 0.x = 0 (module-zero-act).
  • mal-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • mal-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • ring-carrier-closed-mul -- warrant proof: MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
  • finsum-congruence -- warrant well-known: If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)= FINSUM(ag,g,S). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH. Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
  • mar-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • mar-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • mar-gj-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • finsum-act-distrib-gen -- warrant well-known: r.(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r . f z) in a module: x |-> r.x is an endomorphism of the vector abelian group. Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: r.(SUM_X f + f z0) = r.SUM_X f + r.(f z0) by module-act-distrib-vec, and the empty case is r.0 = 0, itself r.(0+0) = r.0 + r.0 killed by abelian-group-idempotent-is-id on the vector group (the module-zero-act route).
  • mar-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • mar-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into the scalar ring's carrier / the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure + module-act-type; mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) with VEC md).
  • matact-assoc-summand-type -- warrant well-known: (c,j) |-> (P_{row,j} * Q_{j,c}) . u_{c,col} is a function on [1,k] x [1,n] into the vector abelian group's carrier (entry-in-carrier, ring MUL closure, module-act-type, mvag-carr).
  • finsum-fubini-c -- warrant well-known: finsum-fubini with curried set/finiteness premises (AND-packaged), for fact-friendly forward use.
  • matrix-entry-extensionality -- warrant well-known: Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
  • tel-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-inf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • finsum-ring-distrib-right-gen -- warrant well-known: (SUM_z f z) * r = SUM_z (f z * r) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert with ring-right-dist; a |-> a*r is an additive-group endomorphism.
  • tel-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).

  • ter-gj-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • finsum-ring-distrib-left-gen -- warrant well-known: r*(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r * f z) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert, r(SUM_X f + f z0) = rSUM_X f + r*f z0 by ring-left-dist, then the IH. No commutativity used (a |-> r*a is an additive-group endomorphism).
  • ter-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • matmul-assoc-summand-type -- warrant well-known: the triple-product summand (c,j) |-> (P_{row,j} Q_{j,c}) R_{c,col} is a function INTERVAL(1,k) x INTERVAL(1,n) -> CARR A: the entries lie in CARR A (entry-in-carrier) and MUL closes on CARR A.
  • identmat-right-identity -- warrant proof: P I_n = P, the right-handed mirror of identmat-left-identity (only the c=j term of sum_j P_ij delta_jc survives).

sqrt2-irrational (trust: none)

trusted code: arith, crs, ineq -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

  • nn-mul-succ -- warrant reference: The recursion equation defining * on NN: a * succ(b) = a*b + a. Peano. Same gap as nn-add-succ: number-systems.scm gives * its algebraic laws and never relates it to succ.
  • nn-add-succ -- warrant reference: The recursion equation defining + on NN: a + succ(b) = succ(a + b). Peano. ASSERTED because number-systems.scm axiomatizes NN's + by its algebraic laws (comm/assoc/distrib/zero) and never relates it to succ, leaving the two symbols unconnected. Retire it by constructing + as the recursion it is.
  • nn-zero-le -- warrant well-known: 0 is the least natural number.
  • nn-le-imp-neq-succ -- warrant well-known: j <= k => j /= succ k for j,k in NN (succ k > k >= j).
  • zz-is-integral-domain -- NO WARRANT
  • integral-domain-cancel-zero -- NO WARRANT
  • nn-pos-of-nonzero -- warrant well-known: k in NN, k/=0 => 0<k: 0<=k (nn-zero-le) and 0/=k give the strict inequality.
  • co-eq-lt-trans -- warrant well-known: a=b then b<c gives a<c.
  • co-lt-eq-trans -- warrant well-known: a<b then b=c gives a<c.
  • rr-lt-le-trans -- warrant well-known: x < y <= z gives x < z (transitivity; x=z would force y=x against x<y).

submodule-fg (trust: none)

trusted code: crs -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

  • one-in-interval-1 -- warrant proof: 1 in INTERVAL(1,1): 1 in NN and 1<=1<=1. The column index of a column vector.
  • entry-in-carrier -- warrant well-known: The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
  • nn-zero-le -- warrant well-known: 0 is the least natural number.
  • matof-in-mat -- warrant well-known: The tabulated matrix MATOF(m,n,g) is an m-by-n matrix over X when every value g(i,j) (1<=i<=m, 1<=j<=n) lies in X.
  • nn-not-le-zero-pos -- warrant well-known: 1 <= j => not(j <= 0) for j in NN (0 is least, and j >= 1 > 0).
  • matact-entry -- warrant reference: (P.u)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij} . u_{jc}, summed in the module's vector abelian group; the read-off of MATACT's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof (dimensions m, n, q recovered from SIZE P, SIZE u).
  • interval-1-0-empty -- warrant well-known: [1,0] is empty: i in [1,0] gives 1 <= i and i <= 0, hence 1 <= 0, false in NN.
  • finsum-empty -- warrant informal: Mechanically proven before the 2026-05-27 PSS promotion: mac FINSUM exposes SUM-AG over CARD(EMPTY-SET), card-empty rewrites that to 0, and sum-ag-zero collapses the empty sum to the identity. Script archived at archive/proven-theorems-archive.scm (prove-and-install! 'finsum-empty). The archive predates the E -> IDEN accessor rename and the ==-sweep, so it is a record of the argument rather than a runnable script -- which is why this is informal' and not proof'.
  • mvag-id -- warrant proof: identity of a module's vector abelian group is the module's zero vector.
  • nn-one-in -- warrant well-known: 1 = succ 0 in NN.
  • nn-le-succ -- warrant well-known: k <= succ k on NN.
  • nn-one-le-succ -- warrant well-known: 1 <= succ n for every n in NN.
  • interval-mem-intro -- warrant proof: Converse of interval-lo/interval-hi: i in NN with a<=i<=b lies in INTERVAL(a,b) (the right-to-left direction of interval-membership's SEP iff).
  • block-type -- warrant reference: The leading k-by-l block of an m-by-n matrix over X is a k-by-l matrix over X (matof-in-mat; every value ENTRY P i j with i<=k<=m, j<=l<=n lies in X).
  • ring-one-in -- warrant well-known: ONE lies in the carrier.
  • ring-neg-in-carr -- warrant well-known: NEG closes on the carrier (op NEG CARR CARR).
  • zeromat-type -- warrant reference: the all-zero m-by-n matrix is m-by-n over CARR A.
  • interval-card-in-nn -- warrant well-known: INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
  • matact-summand-type -- warrant well-known: j |-> P_{ij} . u_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> VEC md for P:MAT(m,n,CARR(SCAL md)) and u:MAT(n,q,VEC md): entry-in-carrier types both arguments, module-act-type closes the action, and mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
  • entry-of-zeromat -- warrant reference: (ZEROMAT A m n)_{ij} = 0 (entry-of-matof on the constant tabulation).
  • abelian-group-idempotent-is-id -- NO WARRANT
  • finsum-all-id -- warrant well-known: If f(z) = IDEN(ag) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S) = IDEN(ag). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term is IDEN and the rest is IDEN by the IH, so the sum is IDEN*IDEN = IDEN; the base case is finsum-empty.
  • matadd-type -- warrant reference: entrywise sum of m-by-n matrices is m-by-n.
  • mra-combined-summand-type -- warrant well-known: j |-> (c1_{1j}.u_{j1}) (+) (c2_{1j}.u_{j1}) is a function [1,n] -> VEC md: each summand is (matact-summand-type), and the vector abelian group's operation closes on its carrier (mvag-carr, mvag-op, module-vadd-type).
  • matadd-entry -- warrant reference: (P+Q)_{ij} = P_{ij} + Q_{ij}: the read-off of MATADD's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof + beta, with the dimensions recovered from SIZE P (mat-size).
  • mvag-op -- warrant proof: operation of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector addition.
  • finsum-congruence -- warrant well-known: If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)= FINSUM(ag,g,S). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH. Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
  • finsum-add-ag -- warrant well-known: finsum-add at m = ag as a commutative monoid: induction on |S| via finsum-insert-ag, regrouping (a+b)+(c+d)=(a+c)+(b+d) by commutativity and associativity of (OPR ag). No inverses used.
  • matscale-type -- warrant reference: entrywise scalar multiple of an m-by-n matrix is m-by-n (MUL closes on CARR A).
  • mrs-scaled-summand-type -- warrant well-known: z |-> r . (c_{1z} . u_{z1}) is a function [1,n] -> VEC md: the inner action is (matact-summand-type), module-act-type closes the outer action, and mvag-carr identifies CARR(MODULE-VECTOR-AG md) = VEC md.
  • finsum-act-distrib-gen -- warrant well-known: r.(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r . f z) in a module: x |-> r.x is an endomorphism of the vector abelian group. Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: r.(SUM_X f + f z0) = r.SUM_X f + r.(f z0) by module-act-distrib-vec, and the empty case is r.0 = 0, itself r.(0+0) = r.0 + r.0 killed by abelian-group-idempotent-is-id on the vector group (the module-zero-act route).
  • matscale-entry -- warrant reference: (r*P)_{ij} = r * P_{ij}: the read-off of MATSCALE's MATOF tabulation via entry-of-matof + beta, with the dimensions recovered from SIZE P (mat-size).
  • ring-add-right-inv -- warrant well-known: a + (-a) = 0 (ring-add-left-inv + comm).
  • abelian-group-inverse-unique -- warrant well-known: b = eb = (a^{-1}a)b = a^{-1}(ab) = a^{-1}e = a^{-1}, by group-left-inv, group-assoc, group-left-id, and abelian-group-opr-comm for the right identity. Every abelian group is a group (abelian-group-is-group), so all four are in scope.
  • unitrow-type -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i) is a 1-by-n matrix over CARR A (matof-in-mat; entries ONE/ZERO).
  • unitrow-entry-off -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i)_{1,j} = 0 for j /= i (entry-of-matof with the IF condition false).
  • finsum-single-support -- warrant well-known: If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
  • unitrow-entry-at -- warrant reference: UNITROW(A,n,i)_{1,i} = 1 (entry-of-matof with the IF condition true).
  • ring-add-closed -- warrant well-known: ADD closes on the carrier (curried form).
  • ring-neg-mul-left -- warrant well-known: (-a)b = -(ab): ab + (-a)b = (a + -a)b = 0b = 0 (ring-right-dist, ring-add-left-inv, ring-mul-zero-left), so (-a)b is the inverse of ab.
  • ring-carrier-closed-mul -- warrant proof: MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
  • gauge-is-degree -- warrant proof: GAUGE(s) = CHOICE(EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES s) is in FUN(CARR s, NN) and has division- with-remainder: IS-EUCLIDEAN-RING(s) makes EUCLIDEAN-GAUGES(s) inhabited (euclidean-ring-has-gauge + gauges-mem-build), so the epsilon pick lands in it (choice-axiom) and the SEP slices give both conjuncts. MACHINE-PROVEN in archive/calculus-pre-rename/gauge-proof.scm.
  • principal-ideal-membership -- NO WARRANT
  • ring-add-right-id -- warrant well-known: a + 0 = a (ring-add-left-id + comm).
  • nn-succ-le-antisym -- warrant well-known: succ a <= b => not(b <= a) for a,b in NN: succ a <= b gives a < b, so b <= a would give a < a. Elementary order on NN.
  • ring-mul-zero-left -- warrant well-known: 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group. Lang, Algebra II.1.
  • snoc-col-type -- warrant reference: [w_1..w_n, x] is a (succ n)-by-1 matrix over X: matof-in-mat, each value being x in X (row succ n) or ENTRY w i 1 in X for i in [1,n] (entry-in-carrier).
  • nn-succ-mono -- warrant well-known: a <= b => succ a <= succ b.
  • nn-le-succ-cases -- warrant well-known: j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).
  • entry-of-snoc-col -- warrant reference: SNOC-COL(w,n,x)_{i,1} = w_{i,1} for i in [1,n]: entry-of-matof (i in [1,succ n] since i<=n<=succ n, and 1 in [1,1]), then the IF is false since i<=n<succ n.
  • snoc-col-last -- warrant reference: SNOC-COL(w,n,x)_{succ n,1} = x: entry-of-matof (succ n in [1,succ n]), IF true.
  • matact-type -- warrant reference: P.u is an m-by-q matrix of vectors for P an m-by-n scalar matrix and u an n-by-q matrix of vectors: matof-in-mat, whose values are FINSUMs in the vector abelian group, typed by finsum-type + mvag-carr.
  • matact-summand-type-le -- warrant well-known: j |-> P_{ij} . u_{jc} is a function [1,k] -> VEC md for k <= n: matact-summand- type's argument, restricted -- entry-in-carrier still types both arguments for j in [1,k] subset [1,n] (interval [1,k] subset [1,n] since k <= n).
  • finsum-interval-peel -- warrant well-known: FINSUM over [1,succ n] = (FINSUM over [1,n]) . f(succ n): finsum-insert-ag at X = [1,n], k = succ n, using [1,succ n] = [1,n] u {succ n} (interval-mem-intro / interval-hi + nn-le-succ-cases) and succ n not in [1,n] (interval-hi would give succ n <= n). Standard finite-sum back-peel, the twin of finsum-interval-shift.
  • entry-of-block -- warrant reference: BLOCK(P,k,l)_{ij} = P_{ij} for i in [1,k], j in [1,l] (entry-of-matof).
  • finsum-type -- warrant informal: Closure of the finite sum on the carrier. Induction on card(S): the fold seeds at (IDEN ag), in (CARR ag) by the identity law, and each step applies (OPR ag), which closes on (CARR ag) by the group's binary-operation typing. No inverses used -- the same argument as finsum-comm-monoid-type.
  • mvag-carr -- warrant proof: carrier of a module's vector abelian group is the module's vector set.
  • abelian-group-right-id -- warrant well-known: a * e = e * a = a, by abelian-group-opr-comm then group-left-id (via abelian-group-is-group).
  • abelian-group-assoc -- warrant well-known: (ab)c = a(bc): group-assoc via abelian-group-is-group.
  • snoc-row-type -- warrant reference: [c_1..c_n, r] is a 1-by-(succ n) matrix over X (matof-in-mat, as snoc-col-type).
  • snoc-row-last -- warrant reference: SNOC-ROW(c,n,r)_{1,succ n} = r (entry-of-matof, IF true).
  • entry-of-snoc-row -- warrant reference: SNOC-ROW(c,n,r)_{1,j} = c_{1,j} for j in [1,n] (entry-of-matof, IF false as j<=n<succ n).

submodule-intersection (trust: proof)

proven modulo 0 -- unconditional.

subseq-of-convergent (trust: well-known)

proven modulo:

  • subseq-is-fun -- warrant well-known: f : NN -> PTS(s) and phi : NN -> NN, so the composite SUBSEQ(f,phi) = k |-> f(phi k) is again a function NN -> PTS(s). Pure composition typing.
  • strictly-mono-ge-id -- warrant proof: NN-induction on k. k=0: 0 <= phi(0) (NN is nonnegative). k -> succ k: phi(k) >= k (IH) and phi(succ k) > phi(k) (strict monotonicity at k < succ k), so phi(succ k) > k, i.e. phi(succ k) >= succ k on NN. Standard.

swap-to-corner (trust: well-known)

proven modulo:

  • elem-f-type -- warrant reference: ELEM-F(A,n,k,l) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A; matof-in-mat on the swap entry function).
  • matmul-type -- warrant reference: MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat + finsum-type).
  • identmat-type -- warrant reference: IDENTMAT(A,n) is an n-by-n matrix over CARR A (its entries are ONE/ZERO of A).
  • matmul-entry -- warrant reference: (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
  • interval-card-in-nn -- warrant well-known: INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
  • matprod-summand-type -- warrant well-known: j |-> P_{ij}.Q_{jc} is a function [1,n] -> CARR A for P:MAT(m,n), Q:MAT(n,nn) (entry-in-carrier gives both factors in CARR A; MUL closes; CARR(RAG)=CARR A).
  • elem-f-ck-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{jc} = 0 for c=k, j/=l.
  • entry-in-carrier -- warrant well-known: The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
  • ring-mul-zero-right -- warrant well-known: a.0 = 0: symmetric to ring-mul-zero-left. Lang, Algebra II.1.
  • ras-id -- warrant proof: identity of a ring's additive group is the ring's zero.
  • finsum-single-support -- warrant well-known: If f(j)=0 for every j in the finite S except j=i0, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)=f(i0).
  • elem-f-ck-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{lc} = 1 for c=k.
  • elem-f-cl-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{jc} = 0 for c=l, j/=k.
  • elem-f-cl-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{kc} = 1 for c=l.
  • elem-f-co-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{jc} = 0 for c/=k, c/=l, j/=c.
  • elem-f-co-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{cc} = 1 for c/=k, c/=l.
  • entry-of-identmat -- warrant reference: (IDENTMAT A n)_{ij} = 1 if i=j else 0 (entry-of-matof on the Kronecker delta).
  • elem-f-col-k -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ik} = 1 if i=l else 0 (k/=l).
  • elem-f-col-l -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{il} = 1 if i=k else 0 (k/=l).
  • elem-f-col-other -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ic} = 1 if i=c else 0 (c/=k, c/=l).
  • matrix-entry-extensionality -- warrant well-known: Two m-by-n matrices over X with equal entries are equal (tuple/list extensionality applied row- and entry-wise).
  • identmat-right-identity -- warrant proof: P I_n = P, the right-handed mirror of identmat-left-identity (only the c=j term of sum_j P_ij delta_jc survives).
  • identmat-left-identity -- warrant proof: I_m P = P: the (i,c) entry is sum_j delta_ij P_jc, whose only surviving term (j=i) is 1*P_ic = P_ic (matmul-entry + entry-of-identmat + finsum-single- support + ring-mul-left-id). Proof structure worked out in identmat-build.scm.
  • tel-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-inf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • finsum-ring-distrib-right-gen -- warrant well-known: (SUM_z f z) * r = SUM_z (f z * r) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert with ring-right-dist; a |-> a*r is an additive-group endomorphism.
  • tel-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ring-carrier-closed-mul -- warrant proof: MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
  • finsum-congruence -- warrant well-known: If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)= FINSUM(ag,g,S). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH. Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.
  • ter-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-gj-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • finsum-ring-distrib-left-gen -- warrant well-known: r*(SUM_z f z) = SUM_z (r * f z) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert, r(SUM_X f + f z0) = rSUM_X f + r*f z0 by ring-left-dist, then the IH. No commutativity used (a |-> r*a is an additive-group endomorphism).
  • ter-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ter-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • matmul-assoc-summand-type -- warrant well-known: the triple-product summand (c,j) |-> (P_{row,j} Q_{j,c}) R_{c,col} is a function INTERVAL(1,k) x INTERVAL(1,n) -> CARR A: the entries lie in CARR A (entry-in-carrier) and MUL closes on CARR A.
  • finsum-fubini-c -- warrant well-known: finsum-fubini with curried set/finiteness premises (AND-packaged), for fact-friendly forward use.
  • elem-f-rk-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=k, j/=l.
  • ring-mul-zero-left -- warrant well-known: 0.a = 0: a.0 = a.(0+0) = a.0 + a.0, cancel a.0 in the additive group. Lang, Algebra II.1.
  • elem-f-rk-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{il} = 1 for i=k.
  • elem-f-rl-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i=l, j/=k.
  • elem-f-rl-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ik} = 1 for i=l.
  • elem-f-ro-off -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ij} = 0 for i/=k, i/=l, j/=i.
  • elem-f-ro-at -- warrant reference: F[k,l]_{ii} = 1 for i/=k, i/=l.

taylor-lagrange (trust: none)

trusted code: crs -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

  • taylor-g-in-fun -- warrant reference: G(t)=f(x)-TAYLOR-POLY(f,t,n,x) maps RR to RR (finite sum of products of reals), for each degree n in NN.
  • taylor-h-in-fun -- warrant reference: H(t)=(x-t)^(n+1) maps RR to RR, for each degree n in NN.
  • taylor-gmvt-cont -- warrant reference: G and H are continuous on [a,x]: H is a polynomial, G a finite sum of products of the continuous derivatives f^(k) with polynomials (taylor-G-cont/H-cont).
  • taylor-gmvt-diff -- warrant reference: G and H are differentiable on (a,x) (taylor-G-diff/H-diff give the explicit derivatives), so each has SOME derivative there.
  • nary-times-2 -- NO WARRANT
  • nary-minus-2 -- NO WARRANT
  • binneg-in-fun-rr -- warrant well-known: Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
  • binplus-in-fun-rr -- warrant well-known: Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
  • bintimes-in-fun-rr -- warrant well-known: Closure of a primitive numeric domain under its arithmetic operation; standard.
  • gmvt-aux-cont -- warrant reference: h = (g(b)-g(a))f - (f(b)-f(a))g is a linear combination of f and g, hence continuous wherever both f and g are (calculus.pdf Thm 2.11).
  • gmvt-aux-diff -- warrant reference: h'(x) = (g(b)-g(a))f'(x) - (f(b)-f(a))g'(x), from deriv-sum/scalar-mult on the linear combination h = (g(b)-g(a))f - (f(b)-f(a))g (calculus.pdf Thm 2.11).
  • rr-lt-implies-le -- warrant well-known: x < y is (x <= y and x =/= y) by definition, so in particular x <= y.
  • extreme-value-max -- warrant reference: Extreme Value Theorem (calculus.pdf Ch 2.5, used in Rolle's lemma): a continuous f on the compact [a,b] attains its supremum. Sequentially: a maximizing sequence in [a,b] has a convergent subsequence (seq-compactness of [a,b]); its limit c is in [a,b] and f(c) is the max by sequential continuity.
  • extreme-value-min -- warrant reference: EVT (min form): apply extreme-value-max to -f; the argmax of -f is the argmin of f.
  • ccint-membership -- warrant proof: Separation: x in CCINT(a,b) = SEP(x in RR | a<=x and x<=b) iff x in RR and a<=x and x<=b, by the SEP membership kernel rule.
  • rr-le-cases -- warrant well-known: u<=v => u<v or u=v.
  • max-val-strict-interior -- warrant well-known: c in [a,b], h(a)=h(b), h(a)<h(c) => a<c<b (a strict max above equal endpoints is interior).
  • rr-lt-trans -- warrant well-known: Strict order is transitive: from x<y<z, x<=z by rr-leq-transitive and x=/=z (else y would be both > and < x).
  • rr-lt-diff-pos -- warrant well-known: u<v => 0<v-u.
  • rr-le-diff-nonpos -- warrant well-known: u<=v => u-v<=0.
  • rr-prod-nonpos-pos -- warrant well-known: u*v<=0 with v>0 forces u<=0.
  • rr-lt-diff-neg -- warrant well-known: u<v => u-v<0.
  • rr-prod-nonpos-neg -- warrant well-known: u*v<=0 with v<0 forces u>=0.
  • continuous-nonpos-right -- warrant well-known: A function continuous at th that is <=0 on a right-neighborhood (th,bb) is <=0 at th (sign preservation under continuity / one-sided limit).
  • continuous-nonneg-left -- warrant well-known: A function continuous at th that is >=0 on a left-neighborhood (aa,th) is >=0 at th.
  • min-val-strict-interior -- warrant well-known: c in [a,b], h(a)=h(b), h(c)<h(a) => a<c<b.
  • deriv-neg -- warrant reference: Derivative of -f is -f': f(x)-f(a)=phi(x)(x-a) gives (-f)(x)-(-f)(a) = (-phi)(x)(x-a), with -phi continuous at a and value -L.
  • nary-neg-1 -- NO WARRANT
  • rr-le-neg -- warrant well-known: u<=v => -v<=-u.
  • rr-neg-eq-zero -- warrant well-known: -u=0 => u=0.
  • rr-midpoint-between -- warrant well-known: a<b => exists m with a<m<b.
  • diff-value-real -- warrant informal: IS-DIFF-AT's definition includes (IN L RR) as a conjunct.
  • rr-sub-ne-zero -- warrant well-known: u/=v => u-v/=0.
  • rr-cancel-mul-right -- warrant well-known: uc=vc with c/=0 gives u=v (multiply by 1/c). The right-factor companion of rr-cancel-mul-left (taylor-proof.scm).
  • cont-agree-off-pt -- warrant well-known: fa, fb continuous at pt and fa(x)=fb(x) for all x/=pt => fa(pt)=fb(pt). pt is a limit point of RR (no isolated points), so both values are the common limit of the punctured values; take x -> pt.
  • rr-diff-zero-eq -- warrant well-known: 0=u-v => u=v.
  • rr-strict-between-real -- warrant well-known: If u<x<v with u,v real then x is real: the strict order on RR only relates reals, so an interior point of [u,v] lies in RR.
  • taylor-g-diff -- warrant reference: Telescoping: d/dt[f(x)-Sum_{k<=n} f^(k)(t)(x-t)^k/k!] = -(f^(n+1)(t)/n!)(x-t)^n. Differentiate term k by the product rule: the f^(k+1)(t)(x-t)^k/k! piece of term k cancels the -f^(k+1)(t)(x-t)^k/k! piece of term k+1, leaving only the last -(f^(n+1)(t)/n!)(x-t)^n. Standard (calculus.pdf Taylor section).
  • taylor-h-diff -- warrant reference: d/dt[(x-t)^(n+1)] = -(n+1)(x-t)^n (chain rule on the polynomial).
  • taylor-g-at-x -- warrant reference: G(x) = f(x) - TAYLOR-POLY(f,x,n,x) = f(x) - f(x) = 0 (taylor-poly-at-center).
  • taylor-g-at-a -- warrant reference: G(a) = f(x) - TAYLOR-POLY(f,a,n,x) (beta).
  • taylor-h-at-x -- warrant reference: H(x) = (x-x)^(n+1) = 0^(n+1) = 0.
  • taylor-h-at-a -- warrant reference: H(a) = (x-a)^(n+1) (beta).
  • rr-power-pos -- warrant well-known: 0<d gives 0<d^n for every n (induction: d^0=1>0; d^(n+1)=d*d^n, product of positives).
  • rr-pos-ne-zero -- warrant well-known: 0<c gives c/=0.
  • taylor-deriv-real -- warrant reference: f^(n+1)(t) is the derivative value of the differentiable f^(n) at t, hence real (IS-DIFF-AT carries its value in RR).
  • power-in-rr -- warrant well-known: b^n in RR for b real, n in NN (RR closed under *).
  • taylor-poly-in-rr -- warrant reference: TAYLOR-POLY is a finite sum of products of reals, hence real.
  • taylor-clear -- warrant well-known: Substitute gx=hx=0, ha=d, ga=r: (-(fn1/n!)pw)(-d) = (-(n+1)pw)(-r), i.e. (fn1/n!)pwd = (n+1)pwr; cancel pw/=0 and multiply by n! (using (n+1)*n! = (n+1)!) to get (n+1)! r = fn1 d. Pure real arithmetic.

totally-bounded-has-cauchy-subsequence (trust: none)

trusted code: arith -- decision procedures, sound on their domain but believed rather than checked; they leave no leaf below.

proven modulo:

  • null-rr-seq-exists -- warrant well-known: k |-> 2^-k is in FUN(NN,RR), is positive, and 2^-k -> 0 (Archimedean / geometric decay): given eps>0 pick N with 2^-N <= eps. A concrete witness; no content.
  • tb-rad-ball-cover -- warrant reference: cov(k) := IMAGE(c |-> BALL(s,c,rad k), F_k), F_k a finite rad(k)-net (TOTALLY-BOUNDED at rad k > 0; CHOICE picks one per k). (i) cov(k) is a finite cover of PTS(s): image of the finite F_k is finite, and IS-R-NET makes every p in PTS(s) lie within rad(k) of some c in F_k, i.e. p in BALL(s,c,rad k) in cov(k) (IS-R-NET uses d<r, matching the open ball, so the balls truly cover). (ii) every member is by construction BALL(s,c,rad k) with c in F_k subset PTS(s) (centres in PTS(s) by DIST(s) typing). Also hands back (PTS s) in SET (free from IS-METRIC-SPACE s), the set V the combinatorial engine needs. The metric content total boundedness contributes to block-family, isolated in one bridge -- see archive/calculus-pre-rename/cauchy-subseq-via-combinatorial.scm.
  • cover-block-step -- warrant well-known: Infinite pigeonhole behind a finite cover. C finite covers V, so CHOICE gives a classifier pi:J->C with f(i) in pi(i) for i in J; pigeonhole- infinite on the infinite J then yields a member U in C with an infinite fibre J_ = SEP(i in J | pi(i)=U) subset J, and f(i) in U for every i in J_. Metric-free generalization of tb-block-step (which is this at C = a finite rad-net's ball cover).
  • inf-subsets-is-set -- NO WARRANT
  • nn-in-inf-subsets -- warrant well-known: NN is an infinite subset of NN: NN subset NN and CARD(NN) not in NN.
  • dc-on-nn-pred -- warrant reference: Set-valued-step form of dc-on-nn (same file). Derivable from primitive recursion on NN + global Hilbert choice: g(k,u):=CHOICE{y in X:y in nxt (k,u)} is defined by the totality hypothesis, then recurse f(0):=a, f(succ k):=g(k,f k); f:NN->X by induction. No relation-set encoding -- nxt(k,u) is the successor SET, supplied as a VNB-LAMBDA, so callers avoid the (LIST k u y)-in-R comprehension/sethood/NTH plumbing (O1 of archive/calculus-pre-rename/block-family-rederive.scm). Accepted asserted during library-build.
  • inf-subset-nn-unbounded -- warrant well-known: An infinite subset T of NN is unbounded: if every y in T satisfied y <= u, then T subset {0,...,u} = ORD-SEGMENT(succ u), a finite set, forcing CARD T in NN and contradicting T in INF-SUBSETS(NN). Hence some y in T has u < y; and y in NN since T subset NN. The contrapositive 'bounded subset of NN is finite'.
  • nn-step-strictly-mono -- warrant well-known: g:NN->NN with g(k) < g(succ k) for all k is strictly monotone. For m < n, write n reached from m by succ-steps and chain g(m) < g(m+1) < ... < g(n) by transitivity of < (rr-lt-trans); formally NN-induction on n using m < succ n iff m <= n. STRICTLY-MONO-NN also wants g in FUN(NN,NN), supplied as the hypothesis. Textbook.
  • nn-one-le-succ -- warrant well-known: 1 <= succ n for every n in NN.
  • nn-not-le-zero-pos -- warrant well-known: 1 <= j => not(j <= 0) for j in NN (0 is least, and j >= 1 > 0).
  • nn-le-succ-cases -- warrant well-known: j <= succ k => j <= k or j = succ k, for j,k in NN (discreteness of NN).
  • subseq-is-fun -- warrant well-known: f : NN -> PTS(s) and phi : NN -> NN, so the composite SUBSEQ(f,phi) = k |-> f(phi k) is again a function NN -> PTS(s). Pure composition typing.
  • rr-pos-halvable -- warrant well-known: Every positive real eps can be halved: there is a positive d with d + d = eps. Standard (d = eps/2).
  • cauchy-block-estimate -- warrant proof: y,z in U = BALL(s,c,r): substitute U, ball-2r-triangle gives d(y,z) <= r+r. r <= d gives r+r <= d+d (rr-le-add), and d+d = eps, so r+r <= eps; rr-le-trans then gives d(y,z) <= eps. Curried (no AND antecedents) so the headline proof discharges every premise by in-context `fact' auto-detach.

triple-entry-left (trust: well-known)

proven modulo:

  • matmul-type -- warrant reference: MAT(m,n,CARR A) x MAT(n,k,CARR A) -> MAT(m,k,CARR A): the tabulated product is a matrix whose entries are FINSUMs in the carrier (matof-in-mat + finsum-type).
  • matmul-entry -- warrant reference: (P Q)_{ic} = sum_{j=1}^{n} P_{ij}*Q_{jc}, the product summed in A's additive group over j in 1..n (entry-of-matof on the MATMUL tabulation).
  • interval-card-in-nn -- warrant well-known: INTERVAL(a,b) is finite, so its cardinality is a natural number.
  • tel-outf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-tout-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-inf-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • entry-in-carrier -- warrant well-known: The (i,j) entry of an m-by-n matrix over X lies in X.
  • finsum-ring-distrib-right-gen -- warrant well-known: (SUM_z f z) * r = SUM_z (f z * r) in an arbitrary ring: induction on |S| via finsum-insert with ring-right-dist; a |-> a*r is an additive-group endomorphism.
  • tel-dist-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • tel-red-type -- warrant well-known: the summand lambda is a function into CARR A / the additive AG's carrier (entry-in-carrier + MUL closure).
  • ring-carrier-closed-mul -- warrant proof: MUL closes on CARR: (op MUL) has type CARR x CARR -> CARR (fun-apply-type).
  • finsum-congruence -- warrant well-known: If f(z)=g(z) for every z in the finite index set S, then FINSUM(ag,f,S)= FINSUM(ag,g,S). Induction on |S| via finsum-insert: the peeled term agrees (f z0 = g z0) and the rest by the IH. Equivalently, FINSUM depends only on the restriction of the summand to S, so it factors through pointwise equality.

  • dual-norm-on-nonneg -- warrant reference: DUAL-NORM-ON is defined by IOTA over c in RR with 0<=c, so for a bounded functional it is a nonnegative real.
  • hb-gap -- warrant reference: Hahn-Banach gap (real, one dimension): with M = ||f||_s, for z,w in s f(w-z) <= M||w-z|| <= M(||z+v||+||w+v||), so sup_z(-f(z)-M||z+v||) <= inf_w(-f(w)+M||w+v||); RR order-completeness gives a separating alpha, whence |f(y)+r.alpha| <= M||y+r.v|| for all y in s, r in RR.
  • hb-extend-construct -- warrant reference: Extending a linear functional one dimension: with v notin s every element of s + RR.v is uniquely y + r.v, so g(y+r.v) = f(y) + r.alpha is a well-defined linear functional on s + RR.v extending f, for any scalar alpha. Standard linear algebra; alpha is fixed only by the bound (hb-gap).
  • span-add-one-membership -- warrant proof: Separation membership: SPAN-ADD-ONE(m,s,v) = SEP(w in VEC(m) | exists y in s, r in RR. w = y + r.v); a member iff in VEC(m) and it so decomposes.
  • span-add-one-submodule -- warrant reference: t + RR.v is closed under addition, negation and the scalar action and contains 0, so it is a submodule.
  • span-add-one-superset -- warrant reference: Every y in t equals y + 0.v, so t is contained in t + RR.v.
  • extends-on-trans -- warrant reference: If g2=g1 on t, g1=f on s, and s subset t, then g2=f on s.
  • vnrm-real -- warrant reference: VNRM(m) : VEC(m) -> RR, so the norm of a vector is a real.
  • vnrm-nonneg -- warrant reference: Norm nonnegativity, a defining law of IS-NORMED-VECTOR-SPACE.

  • dual-norm-on-le-bound -- warrant reference: DUAL-NORM-ON(m,s,g) is the least c>=0 bounding |g(w)| by c*||w|| on s (IOTA least-upper-bound), hence <= any such bound c.
  • le-bound-mono -- warrant well-known: Reals: from p<=an, a<=b and 0<=n, scale a<=b by n>=0 to an<=b*n, then transitivity gives p<=b*n.
  • span-add-one-has-v -- warrant reference: v = 0 + 1.v with 0 in t, so v is in t + RR.v.

  • dual-norm-le-bound -- warrant reference: DUAL-NORM(m,f) is the least c>=0 bounding |f(w)| by c*||w|| on VEC(m) (IOTA least-upper-bound), hence <= any such bound c.
  • dual-norm-nonneg -- warrant reference: DUAL-NORM is defined by IOTA over c in RR with 0<=c, so for a bounded f it is a nonnegative real.
  • line-has-v -- warrant reference: v = 1.v, so v is in RR.v = LINE(m,v).
  • gof-in-fun -- warrant reference: g : VEC(m) -> RR (bounded linear functional) composed with f : RR -> VEC(m) is g o f : RR -> RR.
  • gof-taylor-diff -- warrant reference: A bounded linear functional g is linear and continuous, so it commutes with the Caratheodory derivative: if f^(k) is norm-continuous / vector-differentiable with factor phi, then (g o f)^(k) = g o f^(k) is continuous / differentiable with factor g o phi. Hence g o f is scalar-Taylor-differentiable to order n.
  • taylor-g-in-fun -- warrant reference: G(t)=f(x)-TAYLOR-POLY(f,t,n,x) maps RR to RR (finite sum of products of reals), for each degree n in NN.

  • extreme-value-max -- warrant reference: Extreme Value Theorem (calculus.pdf Ch 2.5, used in Rolle's lemma): a continuous f on the compact [a,b] attains its supremum. Sequentially: a maximizing sequence in [a,b] has a convergent subsequence (seq-compactness of [a,b]); its limit c is in [a,b] and f(c) is the max by sequential continuity.
  • extreme-value-min -- warrant reference: EVT (min form): apply extreme-value-max to -f; the argmax of -f is the argmin of f.

  • continuous-nonneg-left -- warrant well-known: A function continuous at th that is >=0 on a left-neighborhood (aa,th) is >=0 at th.

  • cont-agree-off-pt -- warrant well-known: fa, fb continuous at pt and fa(x)=fb(x) for all x/=pt => fa(pt)=fb(pt). pt is a limit point of RR (no isolated points), so both values are the common limit of the punctured values; take x -> pt.
  • rr-diff-zero-eq -- warrant well-known: 0=u-v => u=v.
  • rr-strict-between-real -- warrant well-known: If u<x<v with u,v real then x is real: the strict order on RR only relates reals, so an interior point of [u,v] lies in RR.
  • taylor-g-diff -- warrant reference: Telescoping: d/dt[f(x)-Sum_{k<=n} f^(k)(t)(x-t)^k/k!] = -(f^(n+1)(t)/n!)(x-t)^n. Differentiate term k by the product rule: the f^(k+1)(t)(x-t)^k/k! piece of term k cancels the -f^(k+1)(t)(x-t)^k/k! piece of term k+1, leaving only the last -(f^(n+1)(t)/n!)(x-t)^n. Standard (calculus.pdf Taylor section).
  • taylor-h-diff -- warrant reference: d/dt[(x-t)^(n+1)] = -(n+1)(x-t)^n (chain rule on the polynomial).
  • taylor-g-at-x -- warrant reference: G(x) = f(x) - TAYLOR-POLY(f,x,n,x) = f(x) - f(x) = 0 (taylor-poly-at-center).
  • taylor-g-at-a -- warrant reference: G(a) = f(x) - TAYLOR-POLY(f,a,n,x) (beta).
  • taylor-h-at-x -- warrant reference: H(x) = (x-x)^(n+1) = 0^(n+1) = 0.
  • taylor-h-at-a -- warrant reference: H(a) = (x-a)^(n+1) (beta).
  • rr-power-pos -- warrant well-known: 0<d gives 0<d^n for every n (induction: d^0=1>0; d^(n+1)=d*d^n, product of positives).
  • rr-pos-ne-zero -- warrant well-known: 0<c gives c/=0.
  • taylor-deriv-real -- warrant reference: f^(n+1)(t) is the derivative value of the differentiable f^(n) at t, hence real (IS-DIFF-AT carries its value in RR).
  • power-in-rr -- warrant well-known: b^n in RR for b real, n in NN (RR closed under *).
  • taylor-poly-in-rr -- warrant reference: TAYLOR-POLY is a finite sum of products of reals, hence real.
  • taylor-clear -- warrant well-known: Substitute gx=hx=0, ha=d, ga=r: (-(fn1/n!)pw)(-d) = (-(n+1)pw)(-r), i.e. (fn1/n!)pwd = (n+1)pwr; cancel pw/=0 and multiply by n! (using (n+1)*n! = (n+1)!) to get (n+1)! r = fn1 d. Pure real arithmetic.
  • g-of-remainder -- warrant reference: g linear: g(f(x) (-) p) = g(f(x)) - g(p) = (g o f)(x) - g(TAYLOR-POLY-V). g commutes with the finite VADD/ACT sum and with f^(k)(a), so g(TAYLOR-POLY-V(f,a,x,n)) = Sum ((x-a)^k/k!) g(f^(k)(a)) = Sum ((x-a)^k/k!) (g o f)^(k)(a) = TAYLOR-POLY(g o f, a, n, x).
  • gof-nth-deriv -- warrant reference: By induction on k using commutation 1's factor identity, (g o f)^(k) = g o f^(k) as functions; evaluating the (n+1)-st at t gives (g o f)^(n+1)(t) = g(f^(n+1)(t)).
  • nth-deriv-v-in-vec -- warrant reference: Each vector derivative f^(k) maps RR into VEC(m) (IS-DIFF-AT-V pins the value in VEC(m)); so f^(k)(t) is a vector.
  • blf-app-real -- warrant reference: A bounded linear functional g maps VEC(m) into RR, so g(v) is a real.
  • dual-norm-is-bound -- warrant reference: The operator norm DUAL-NORM(m,f) satisfies |f(x)| <= ||f|| ||x|| for every x (it is defined as the least such bound; in particular it IS a bound).
  • bdd-linfun-abs-real -- warrant reference: A bounded linear functional maps VEC(m) into RR, so |f(x)| is a real.
  • rr-le-abs-self -- warrant well-known: c <= |c| for real c.
  • rr-mul-le-right -- warrant well-known: a<=b and 0<=c give ac <= bc.
